People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well… my wife kind of made me come in. She got scared because I keep hearing this water running, like a faucet’s on somewhere, and sometimes men whispering inside the walls. I keep checking, you know, like under the sink, around the bathroom—our house is old, the pipes have always been weird, my dad used to say you can tell a lot about a house by the noises it makes—but there’s nobody there.

And sometimes I’ll see this dark, wet-looking patch spreading across the hallway floor, like water seeping out, but when I go over and touch it, it’s dry. That’s… that’s what finally made her say I needed to talk to someone.

It’s mostly the water… the sound of water running, like somebody left a tap open somewhere. I’ll get up and check the sink, the bathroom, the pipes—my father was always fixing pipes when I was a kid, so I notice those noises—but there won’t be anything.

It looks like this dark, wet spot spreading out on the hallway floor, like the wood’s soaking up water, and I’ll think, oh great, the pipes finally went—my dad used to warn me about old houses doing that kind of thing—but then I touch it and it’s dry.\n\nThe rest is more hearing it… the water running when no faucet’s on, and those men whispering inside the walls when there’s nobody there. That’s what scared my wife, I think.\n\n[Last Question]\nAre the sounds of water running and men whispering in the walls happening all the time, or do they come and go?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 49 / not_in_workforce / associates\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Hallucinations\nmanifestation: \"What finally scared my wife is that I keep hearing water running and men whispering inside the walls when nobody’s there, and sometimes I see a dark wet patch spreading across the hallway even after I’ve checked and it’s dry.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
They come and go… I mean, it’s not every single second, but it happens enough that I’m always kind of listening for it. The water running, especially—that’ll start up and I’ll go check the faucets, the bathroom, under the sink, all that. Old houses make noises, sure, my dad used to go on about pipes settling and pressure and all that, but this feels… different.

And the whispering, the men in the walls, that comes and goes too. Sometimes it’s quiet and then suddenly it’s like they’re back there again. That’s what finally scared my wife, I think—me checking and checking when nobody’s there, and then that dark wet-looking patch I see on the hallway floor sometimes, even though when I touch it, it’s dry.

It’s… it’s not like a clock thing, no. I can’t say it’s always at night or after dinner or anything like that. It seems worse when I’m already feeling low, when the house stuff is on my mind and I’m just sitting there thinking how I’ve let everything go.

That’s what scared my wife, I think.\n\nDoctor: Are the sounds of water running and men whispering in the walls happening all the time, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: They come and go… I mean, it’s not every single second, but it happens enough that I’m always kind of listening for it. The water running, especially—that’ll start up and I’ll go check the faucets, the bathroom, under the sink, all that. Old houses make noises, sure, my dad used to go on about pipes settling and pressure and all that, but this feels… different.\n\nAnd the whispering, the men in the walls, that comes and goes too. Sometimes it’s quiet and then suddenly it’s like they’re back there again. That’s what finally scared my wife, I think—me checking and checking when nobody’s there, and then that dark wet-looking patch I see on the hallway floor sometimes, even though when I touch it, it’s dry.\n\nDoctor: Do these sounds and visions happen at any particular time or when you're feeling a certain way?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s not like a clock thing, no. I can’t say it’s always at night or after dinner or anything like that. It seems worse when I’m already feeling low, when the house stuff is on my mind and I’m just sitting there thinking how I’ve let everything go.\n\nBefore all that dragged on—before I got like this, before I stopped caring about the things I used to keep up with—I didn’t hear men in the walls or see those wet spots. I didn’t think people next door were watching me or tracking me. That all came after I already felt like I’d failed at everything.\n\nI don’t know… my dad used to say houses tell you when something’s wrong, pipes knocking and all that, and I keep thinking maybe I should’ve caught it sooner. But it wasn’t there before. Not like this.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the sounds and visions stop or change when your mood improves?\n\nPatient: I think so… or, well, I don’t have a lot of good days to compare it to lately. But before the house problems dragged on and I got this low, I wasn’t hearing voices in the walls or seeing those wet-looking patches that weren’t really wet.\n\nIt all showed up after I’d already kind of stopped caring about things—my usual stuff, the house, everything—and started feeling like I’d failed every single day. Before that, I didn’t think the neighbors were tracking me either. That all came later. So… yeah, I guess when my mood’s worse, that’s when it seems to be there.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Yeah… I do. I mean, how could I not? When I hear that water running, I start thinking something’s leaking somewhere, and I go checking the faucets, the walls, the hallway… and then sometimes there’s that dark wet-looking patch spreading across the floor, but when I touch it, it’s dry.

I don’t think so… not exactly. I mean, I get kind of out of it sometimes, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as being outside my body.

That’s… that’s what finally made her say I needed to talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Are the whispers you hear inside the walls the only thing you've been hearing, or have you been hearing any other sounds that aren't there?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly the water… the sound of water running, like somebody left a tap open somewhere. I’ll get up and check the sink, the bathroom, the pipes—my father was always fixing pipes when I was a kid, so I notice those noises—but there won’t be anything.\n\nAnd then there are the men whispering in the walls, yeah. I can’t always make out what they’re saying, but it’s like they’re back there. My wife says she doesn’t hear it. That’s what scared her, especially when I told her about the dark wet-looking patch I sometimes see on the hallway floor… but when I touch it, it’s dry.\n\nDoctor: Are the whispers and the sound of water the only things you're experiencing that aren't really there, or are you seeing things that aren't there besides the dark patch on the floor?\n\nPatient: No, not really besides that patch… I mean, that’s the main thing I see. It looks like this dark, wet spot spreading out on the hallway floor, like the wood’s soaking up water, and I’ll think, oh great, the pipes finally went—my dad used to warn me about old houses doing that kind of thing—but then I touch it and it’s dry.\n\nThe rest is more hearing it… the water running when no faucet’s on, and those men whispering inside the walls when there’s nobody there. That’s what scared my wife, I think.\n\nDoctor: Are the sounds of water running and men whispering in the walls happening all the time, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: They come and go… I mean, it’s not every single second, but it happens enough that I’m always kind of listening for it. The water running, especially—that’ll start up and I’ll go check the faucets, the bathroom, under the sink, all that. Old houses make noises, sure, my dad used to go on about pipes settling and pressure and all that, but this feels… different.\n\nAnd the whispering, the men in the walls, that comes and goes too. Sometimes it’s quiet and then suddenly it’s like they’re back there again. That’s what finally scared my wife, I think—me checking and checking when nobody’s there, and then that dark wet-looking patch I see on the hallway floor sometimes, even though when I touch it, it’s dry.\n\nDoctor: Do these sounds and visions happen at any particular time or when you're feeling a certain way?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s not like a clock thing, no. I can’t say it’s always at night or after dinner or anything like that. It seems worse when I’m already feeling low, when the house stuff is on my mind and I’m just sitting there thinking how I’ve let everything go.\n\nBefore all that dragged on—before I got like this, before I stopped caring about the things I used to keep up with—I didn’t hear men in the walls or see those wet spots. I didn’t think people next door were watching me or tracking me. That all came after I already felt like I’d failed at everything.\n\nI don’t know… my dad used to say houses tell you when something’s wrong, pipes knocking and all that, and I keep thinking maybe I should’ve caught it sooner. But it wasn’t there before. Not like this.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the sounds and visions stop or change when your mood improves?\n\nPatient: I think so… or, well, I don’t have a lot of good days to compare it to lately. But before the house problems dragged on and I got this low, I wasn’t hearing voices in the walls or seeing those wet-looking patches that weren’t really wet.\n\nIt all showed up after I’d already kind of stopped caring about things—my usual stuff, the house, everything—and started feeling like I’d failed every single day. Before that, I didn’t think the neighbors were tracking me either. That all came later. I mean, how could I not? When I hear that water running, I start thinking something’s leaking somewhere, and I go checking the faucets, the walls, the hallway… and then sometimes there’s that dark wet-looking patch spreading across the floor, but when I touch it, it’s dry.\n\nThe whispering is worse in a way. Men in the walls… nobody’s there, but I hear it, and that’s what really scared my wife, I think. Me stopping everything to listen, checking again and again. It makes me tense, like I’m waiting for something to happen.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that happens right before you start hearing the water running or the men whispering in the walls?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there’s one specific thing right before it. Sometimes I’m just standing there in the hallway, or checking something because I thought I heard a drip, and then it’s like the water is running somewhere in the walls. And then I’ll hear the men whispering… like inside there, but nobody’s there.\n\nWhat scared my wife, I think, was me stopping and listening like that, checking over and over. And sometimes I see this dark wet patch spreading across the hallway floor, but when I go touch it, it’s dry. It’s always dry. Which doesn’t make sense, because it looks wet. I guess I’m not sure. Nothing specific comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Has anything upsetting or disturbing happened to you recently that you think might be connected to these experiences?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, maybe the house. man, and that heaviness just sits on me until I go to bed.\n- key: S025\n  name: Loss_of_Interest\n  description: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.\n  manifestation: I used to be the guy who showed up for soccer, brewed beer in the garage, and helped at the fire department, but now all of it feels pointless and I can’t make myself care enough to go.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: Food mostly tastes like cardboard, so I’ll skip meals without noticing and my jeans have gotten loose even though I’m not trying to lose weight.\n- key: S028\n  name: Psychomotor_Changes\n  description: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.\n  manifestation: My wife says I move like I’m underwater now, and even answering a simple question about a repair estimate can take me so long that people start talking for me.\n- key: S029\n  name: Fatigue\n  description: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.\n  manifestation: Just walking from the kitchen to the back room to look at the tools can wear me out, and resting on the couch doesn’t bring the energy back.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the major depressive episode\n  manifestation: Before the house problems dragged on and I fell into this depression, I never heard voices in the walls, saw wet patches that weren’t there, or thought the neighbors were tracking me. Those things only showed up after I had already stopped caring about my usual life and started feeling like a failure every day.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I’ve stopped keeping up with basic repairs, bills, and commitments, and my wife has had to handle decisions I used to manage myself. Things got bad enough that she called a crisis team because I couldn’t cooperate with a safety inspection at the house.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 7 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about seven months, since the winter freeze turned the house repairs into a mess I couldn’t get out from under.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 49 / not_in_workforce / associates\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S020\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant traumatic events in your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, “traumatic” is a big word. I wouldn’t know what counts exactly. I guess I’m not sure. Nothing specific comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Has anything upsetting or disturbing happened to you recently that you think might be connected to these experiences?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, maybe the house. The winter freeze, that’s when everything turned into this mess with repairs, and I couldn’t get out from under it. It’s been, what, about seven months now?\n\nI keep thinking if I’d handled it better, or if I knew more like my dad did with fixing things, maybe it wouldn’t have gotten so bad. But it was after that freeze, after the house stuff started piling up, that all this really seemed to start.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were outside of your body watching yourself, or like you were in a dream?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not exactly. I mean, I get kind of out of it sometimes, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as being outside my body.\n\nLike, I’ll be standing there and realize I’ve been staring too long, and then I start thinking about how I used to do that as a kid in school, just looking at the clock and losing track, and the teacher would call on me and I’d have no idea what page we were on. I’m not sure… I don’t think I’d say “in a dream,” exactly. Detached, maybe if you mean kind of spaced out, but not like the world isn’t real or I’m not in it.

But I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I haven’t noticed anything like being separate from the world, no.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… my wife kind of made me come in. She got scared because I keep hearing this water running, like a faucet’s on somewhere, and sometimes men whispering inside the walls. I keep checking, you know, like under the sink, around the bathroom—our house is old, the pipes have always been weird, my dad used to say you can tell a lot about a house by the noises it makes—but there’s nobody there.\n\nAnd sometimes I’ll see this dark, wet-looking patch spreading across the hallway floor, like water seeping out, but when I go over and touch it, it’s dry. That’s… that’s what finally made her say I needed to talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Are the whispers you hear inside the walls the only thing you've been hearing, or have you been hearing any other sounds that aren't there?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly the water… the sound of water running, like somebody left a tap open somewhere. I’ll get up and check the sink, the bathroom, the pipes—my father was always fixing pipes when I was a kid, so I notice those noises—but there won’t be anything.\n\nAnd then there are the men whispering in the walls, yeah. I can’t always make out what they’re saying, but it’s like they’re back there. My wife says she doesn’t hear it. That’s what scared her, especially when I told her about the dark wet-looking patch I sometimes see on the hallway floor… but when I touch it, it’s dry.\n\nDoctor: Are the whispers and the sound of water the only things you're experiencing that aren't really there, or are you seeing things that aren't there besides the dark patch on the floor?\n\nPatient: No, not really besides that patch… I mean, that’s the main thing I see. It looks like this dark, wet spot spreading out on the hallway floor, like the wood’s soaking up water, and I’ll think, oh great, the pipes finally went—my dad used to warn me about old houses doing that kind of thing—but then I touch it and it’s dry.\n\nThe rest is more hearing it… the water running when no faucet’s on, and those men whispering inside the walls when there’s nobody there. That’s what scared my wife, I think.\n\nDoctor: Are the sounds of water running and men whispering in the walls happening all the time, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: They come and go… I mean, it’s not every single second, but it happens enough that I’m always kind of listening for it. The water running, especially—that’ll start up and I’ll go check the faucets, the bathroom, under the sink, all that. Old houses make noises, sure, my dad used to go on about pipes settling and pressure and all that, but this feels… different.\n\nAnd the whispering, the men in the walls, that comes and goes too. Sometimes it’s quiet and then suddenly it’s like they’re back there again. That’s what finally scared my wife, I think—me checking and checking when nobody’s there, and then that dark wet-looking patch I see on the hallway floor sometimes, even though when I touch it, it’s dry.\n\nDoctor: Do these sounds and visions happen at any particular time or when you're feeling a certain way?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s not like a clock thing, no. I can’t say it’s always at night or after dinner or anything like that. It seems worse when I’m already feeling low, when the house stuff is on my mind and I’m just sitting there thinking how I’ve let everything go.\n\nBefore all that dragged on—before I got like this, before I stopped caring about the things I used to keep up with—I didn’t hear men in the walls or see those wet spots. I didn’t think people next door were watching me or tracking me. That all came after I already felt like I’d failed at everything.\n\nI don’t know… my dad used to say houses tell you when something’s wrong, pipes knocking and all that, and I keep thinking maybe I should’ve caught it sooner. But it wasn’t there before. Not like this.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the sounds and visions stop or change when your mood improves?\n\nPatient: I think so… or, well, I don’t have a lot of good days to compare it to lately. But before the house problems dragged on and I got this low, I wasn’t hearing voices in the walls or seeing those wet-looking patches that weren’t really wet.\n\nIt all showed up after I’d already kind of stopped caring about things—my usual stuff, the house, everything—and started feeling like I’d failed every single day. Before that, I didn’t think the neighbors were tracking me either. That all came later. So… yeah, I guess when my mood’s worse, that’s when it seems to be there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or stressed when you hear the water running or the men whispering in the walls?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I mean, how could I not? When I hear that water running, I start thinking something’s leaking somewhere, and I go checking the faucets, the walls, the hallway… and then sometimes there’s that dark wet-looking patch spreading across the floor, but when I touch it, it’s dry.\n\nThe whispering is worse in a way. Men in the walls… nobody’s there, but I hear it, and that’s what really scared my wife, I think. Me stopping everything to listen, checking again and again. It makes me tense, like I’m waiting for something to happen.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that happens right before you start hearing the water running or the men whispering in the walls?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there’s one specific thing right before it. Sometimes I’m just standing there in the hallway, or checking something because I thought I heard a drip, and then it’s like the water is running somewhere in the walls. And then I’ll hear the men whispering… like inside there, but nobody’s there.\n\nWhat scared my wife, I think, was me stopping and listening like that, checking over and over. And sometimes I see this dark wet patch spreading across the hallway floor, but when I go touch it, it’s dry. It’s always dry. Which doesn’t make sense, because it looks wet. My dad used to know about these things with houses, leaks and bad pipes, but… I don’t know. I can’t tell what starts it exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant traumatic events in your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, “traumatic” is a big word. I wouldn’t know what counts exactly. I guess I’m not sure. Nothing specific comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Has anything upsetting or disturbing happened to you recently that you think might be connected to these experiences?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, maybe the house. The winter freeze, that’s when everything turned into this mess with repairs, and I couldn’t get out from under it. It’s been, what, about seven months now?\n\nI keep thinking if I’d handled it better, or if I knew more like my dad did with fixing things, maybe it wouldn’t have gotten so bad. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF23, F31.2, F44.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… my wife kind of made me come in. She got scared because I keep hearing this water running, like a faucet’s on somewhere, and sometimes men whispering inside the walls. I keep checking, you know, like under the sink, around the bathroom—our house is old, the pipes have always been weird, my dad used to say you can tell a lot about a house by the noises it makes—but there’s nobody there.\n\nAnd sometimes I’ll see this dark, wet-looking patch spreading across the hallway floor, like water seeping out, but when I go over and touch it, it’s dry. That’s… that’s what finally made her say I needed to talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Are the whispers you hear inside the walls the only thing you've been hearing, or have you been hearing any other sounds that aren't there?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly the water… the sound of water running, like somebody left a tap open somewhere. I’ll get up and check the sink, the bathroom, the pipes—my father was always fixing pipes when I was a kid, so I notice those noises—but there won’t be anything.\n\nAnd then there are the men whispering in the walls, yeah. I can’t always make out what they’re saying, but it’s like they’re back there. My wife says she doesn’t hear it. That’s what scared her, especially when I told her about the dark wet-looking patch I sometimes see on the hallway floor… but when I touch it, it’s dry.\n\nDoctor: Are the whispers and the sound of water the only things you're experiencing that aren't really there, or are you seeing things that aren't there besides the dark patch on the floor?\n\nPatient: No, not really besides that patch… I mean, that’s the main thing I see. It looks like this dark, wet spot spreading out on the hallway floor, like the wood’s soaking up water, and I’ll think, oh great, the pipes finally went—my dad used to warn me about old houses doing that kind of thing—but then I touch it and it’s dry.\n\nThe rest is more hearing it… the water running when no faucet’s on, and those men whispering inside the walls when there’s nobody there. That’s what scared my wife, I think.\n\nDoctor: Are the sounds of water running and men whispering in the walls happening all the time, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: They come and go… I mean, it’s not every single second, but it happens enough that I’m always kind of listening for it. The water running, especially—that’ll start up and I’ll go check the faucets, the bathroom, under the sink, all that. Old houses make noises, sure, my dad used to go on about pipes settling and pressure and all that, but this feels… different.\n\nAnd the whispering, the men in the walls, that comes and goes too. Sometimes it’s quiet and then suddenly it’s like they’re back there again. That’s what finally scared my wife, I think—me checking and checking when nobody’s there, and then that dark wet-looking patch I see on the hallway floor sometimes, even though when I touch it, it’s dry.\n\nDoctor: Do these sounds and visions happen at any particular time or when you're feeling a certain way?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s not like a clock thing, no. I can’t say it’s always at night or after dinner or anything like that. It seems worse when I’m already feeling low, when the house stuff is on my mind and I’m just sitting there thinking how I’ve let everything go.\n\nBefore all that dragged on—before I got like this, before I stopped caring about the things I used to keep up with—I didn’t hear men in the walls or see those wet spots. I didn’t think people next door were watching me or tracking me. That all came after I already felt like I’d failed at everything.\n\nI don’t know… my dad used to say houses tell you when something’s wrong, pipes knocking and all that, and I keep thinking maybe I should’ve caught it sooner. But it wasn’t there before. Not like this.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the sounds and visions stop or change when your mood improves?\n\nPatient: I think so… or, well, I don’t have a lot of good days to compare it to lately. But before the house problems dragged on and I got this low, I wasn’t hearing voices in the walls or seeing those wet-looking patches that weren’t really wet.\n\nIt all showed up after I’d already kind of stopped caring about things—my usual stuff, the house, everything—and started feeling like I’d failed every single day. Before that, I didn’t think the neighbors were tracking me either. That all came later. So… yeah, I guess when my mood’s worse, that’s when it seems to be there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or stressed when you hear the water running or the men whispering in the walls?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I mean, how could I not? When I hear that water running, I start thinking something’s leaking somewhere, and I go checking the faucets, the walls, the hallway… and then sometimes there’s that dark wet-looking patch spreading across the floor, but when I touch it, it’s dry.\n\nThe whispering is worse in a way. Men in the walls… nobody’s there, but I hear it, and that’s what really scared my wife, I think. Me stopping everything to listen, checking again and again. It makes me tense, like I’m waiting for something to happen.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that happens right before you start hearing the water running or the men whispering in the walls?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there’s one specific thing right before it. Sometimes I’m just standing there in the hallway, or checking something because I thought I heard a drip, and then it’s like the water is running somewhere in the walls. And then I’ll hear the men whispering… like inside there, but nobody’s there.\n\nWhat scared my wife, I think, was me stopping and listening like that, checking over and over. And sometimes I see this dark wet patch spreading across the hallway floor, but when I go touch it, it’s dry. It’s always dry. Which doesn’t make sense, because it looks wet. My dad used to know about these things with houses, leaks and bad pipes, but… I don’t know. I can’t tell what starts it exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant traumatic events in your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, “traumatic” is a big word. I wouldn’t know what counts exactly. Nothing like a… I don’t know, some big obvious thing, not that I can point to right now.\n\nMy dad was the kind of person who always knew how to fix things around the house, and I think about that more lately, but that’s not really what you asked. I guess I’m not sure. Nothing specific comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Has anything upsetting or disturbing happened to you recently that you think might be connected to these experiences?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, maybe the house. The winter freeze, that’s when everything turned into this mess with repairs, and I couldn’t get out from under it. That’s… that’s what finally made her say I needed to talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Are the whispers you hear inside the walls the only thing you've been hearing, or have you been hearing any other sounds that aren't there?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly the water… the sound of water running, like somebody left a tap open somewhere. I’ll get up and check the sink, the bathroom, the pipes—my father was always fixing pipes when I was a kid, so I notice those noises—but there won’t be anything.\n\nAnd then there are the men whispering in the walls, yeah. I can’t always make out what they’re saying, but it’s like they’re back there. My wife says she doesn’t hear it. That’s what scared her, especially when I told her about the dark wet-looking patch I sometimes see on the hallway floor… but when I touch it, it’s dry.\n\nDoctor: Are the whispers and the sound of water the only things you're experiencing that aren't really there, or are you seeing things that aren't there besides the dark patch on the floor?\n\nPatient: No, not really besides that patch… I mean, that’s the main thing I see. It looks like this dark, wet spot spreading out on the hallway floor, like the wood’s soaking up water, and I’ll think, oh great, the pipes finally went—my dad used to warn me about old houses doing that kind of thing—but then I touch it and it’s dry.\n\nThe rest is more hearing it… the water running when no faucet’s on, and those men whispering inside the walls when there’s nobody there. That’s what scared my wife, I think.\n\nDoctor: Are the sounds of water running and men whispering in the walls happening all the time, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: They come and go… I mean, it’s not every single second, but it happens enough that I’m always kind of listening for it. The water running, especially—that’ll start up and I’ll go check the faucets, the bathroom, under the sink, all that. Old houses make noises, sure, my dad used to go on about pipes settling and pressure and all that, but this feels… different.\n\nAnd the whispering, the men in the walls, that comes and goes too. Sometimes it’s quiet and then suddenly it’s like they’re back there again. That’s what finally scared my wife, I think—me checking and checking when nobody’s there, and then that dark wet-looking patch I see on the hallway floor sometimes, even though when I touch it, it’s dry.\n\nDoctor: Do these sounds and visions happen at any particular time or when you're feeling a certain way?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s not like a clock thing, no. I can’t say it’s always at night or after dinner or anything like that. It seems worse when I’m already feeling low, when the house stuff is on my mind and I’m just sitting there thinking how I’ve let everything go.\n\nBefore all that dragged on—before I got like this, before I stopped caring about the things I used to keep up with—I didn’t hear men in the walls or see those wet spots. I didn’t think people next door were watching me or tracking me. That all came after I already felt like I’d failed at everything.\n\nI don’t know… my dad used to say houses tell you when something’s wrong, pipes knocking and all that, and I keep thinking maybe I should’ve caught it sooner. But it wasn’t there before. Not like this.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the sounds and visions stop or change when your mood improves?\n\nPatient: I think so… or, well, I don’t have a lot of good days to compare it to lately. But before the house problems dragged on and I got this low, I wasn’t hearing voices in the walls or seeing those wet-looking patches that weren’t really wet.\n\nIt all showed up after I’d already kind of stopped caring about things—my usual stuff, the house, everything—and started feeling like I’d failed every single day. Before that, I didn’t think the neighbors were tracking me either. That all came later. So… yeah, I guess when my mood’s worse, that’s when it seems to be there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or stressed when you hear the water running or the men whispering in the walls?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I mean, how could I not? When I hear that water running, I start thinking something’s leaking somewhere, and I go checking the faucets, the walls, the hallway… and then sometimes there’s that dark wet-looking patch spreading across the floor, but when I touch it, it’s dry.\n\nThe whispering is worse in a way. Men in the walls… nobody’s there, but I hear it, and that’s what really scared my wife, I think. Me stopping everything to listen, checking again and again. It makes me tense, like I’m waiting for something to happen.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that happens right before you start hearing the water running or the men whispering in the walls?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there’s one specific thing right before it. Sometimes I’m just standing there in the hallway, or checking something because I thought I heard a drip, and then it’s like the water is running somewhere in the walls. And then I’ll hear the men whispering… like inside there, but nobody’s there.\n\nWhat scared my wife, I think, was me stopping and listening like that, checking over and over. And sometimes I see this dark wet patch spreading across the hallway floor, but when I go touch it, it’s dry. It’s always dry. Which doesn’t make sense, because it looks wet. My dad used to know about these things with houses, leaks and bad pipes, but… I don’t know. I can’t tell what starts it exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant traumatic events in your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, “traumatic” is a big word. I wouldn’t know what counts exactly. Nothing like a… I don’t know, some big obvious thing, not that I can point to right now.\n\nMy dad was the kind of person who always knew how to fix things around the house, and I think about that more lately, but that’s not really what you asked. I guess I’m not sure. Nothing specific comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Has anything upsetting or disturbing happened to you recently that you think might be connected to these experiences?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, maybe the house. The winter freeze, that’s when everything turned into this mess with repairs, and I couldn’t get out from under it. It’s been, what, about seven months now?\n\nI keep thinking if I’d handled it better, or if I knew more like my dad did with fixing things, maybe it wouldn’t have gotten so bad. But it was after that freeze, after the house stuff started piling up, that all this really seemed to start.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were outside of your body watching yourself, or like you were in a dream?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not exactly. I mean, I get kind of out of it sometimes, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as being outside my body.\n\nLike, I’ll be standing there and realize I’ve been staring too long, and then I start thinking about how I used to do that as a kid in school, just looking at the clock and losing track, and the teacher would call on me and I’d have no idea what page we were on. But that’s different, I guess.\n\nA dream feeling… maybe I don’t know what you mean by that. I haven’t really noticed watching myself from outside or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were in a dream or detached from the world around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think I’d say “in a dream,” exactly. Detached, maybe if you mean kind of spaced out, but not like the world isn’t real or I’m not in it.\n\nSometimes I just get stuck thinking, like my mind wanders off, and then I’m thinking about something completely different—like an errand I forgot or some thing in the house that still needs fixing—and I realize I wasn’t really paying attention. But I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I haven’t noticed anything like being separate from the world, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the problems with the house, like the winter freeze and the repairs, triggered these experiences with the sounds and visions?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it all kind of started feeling tied to that. The freeze, the pipes, all the repairs… and then I’d be sitting there and hear water running when there wasn’t any, or these men whispering, like inside the walls.\n\nThat’s what really scared my wife, I think. And sometimes I see this dark, wet-looking patch spreading in the hallway, and I’ll go check it, and it’s dry. But I still see it for a bit. It makes me feel like the house is still… I don’t know, like it’s not done with me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the sounds and visions would stop if the house repairs were finally completed and everything was back to normal?\n\nPatient: I want to think they would stop, yeah… if the house was just normal again. But I don’t really know. Because even after I check and it’s dry, I still see that dark wet patch spreading down the hallway for a bit.\n\nAnd the sounds… that’s what really scared my wife, I think. I keep hearing water running, or men whispering inside the walls, when there’s nobody there. I tell myself it’s just the repairs or the pipes, but then I go listen and there’s nothing. So… I hope fixing everything would make it stop, but I’m not sure anymore.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Nothing specific comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Has anything upsetting or disturbing happened to you recently that you think might be connected to these experiences?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, maybe the house. The winter freeze, that’s when everything turned into this mess with repairs, and I couldn’t get out from under it. It’s been, what, about seven months now?\n\nI keep thinking if I’d handled it better, or if I knew more like my dad did with fixing things, maybe it wouldn’t have gotten so bad. But it was after that freeze, after the house stuff started piling up, that all this really seemed to start.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were outside of your body watching yourself, or like you were in a dream?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not exactly. It makes me feel like the house is still… I don’t know, like it’s not done with me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the sounds and visions would stop if the house repairs were finally completed and everything was back to normal?\n\nPatient: I want to think they would stop, yeah… if the house was just normal again. But I don’t really know. Because even after I check and it’s dry, I still see that dark wet patch spreading down the hallway for a bit.\n\nAnd the sounds… that’s what really scared my wife, I think. I’m not sure… I don’t know if I’d call it “not connected.” I mean, when it happens I get kind of locked onto it, like everything narrows down to that spot or that sound.

Now it’s like one little thing in the house pulls me in and I lose track of what I was doing. But whether that’s disconnected or just scared… I don’t really know what the difference is.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… my wife kind of made me come in. She got scared because I keep hearing this water running, like a faucet’s on somewhere, and sometimes men whispering inside the walls. I keep checking, you know, like under the sink, around the bathroom—our house is old, the pipes have always been weird, my dad used to say you can tell a lot about a house by the noises it makes—but there’s nobody there.\n\nAnd sometimes I’ll see this dark, wet-looking patch spreading across the hallway floor, like water seeping out, but when I go over and touch it, it’s dry. That’s… that’s what finally made her say I needed to talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Are the whispers you hear inside the walls the only thing you've been hearing, or have you been hearing any other sounds that aren't there?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly the water… the sound of water running, like somebody left a tap open somewhere. I’ll get up and check the sink, the bathroom, the pipes—my father was always fixing pipes when I was a kid, so I notice those noises—but there won’t be anything.\n\nAnd then there are the men whispering in the walls, yeah. I can’t always make out what they’re saying, but it’s like they’re back there. My wife says she doesn’t hear it. That’s what scared her, especially when I told her about the dark wet-looking patch I sometimes see on the hallway floor… but when I touch it, it’s dry.\n\nDoctor: Are the whispers and the sound of water the only things you're experiencing that aren't really there, or are you seeing things that aren't there besides the dark patch on the floor?\n\nPatient: No, not really besides that patch… I mean, that’s the main thing I see. It looks like this dark, wet spot spreading out on the hallway floor, like the wood’s soaking up water, and I’ll think, oh great, the pipes finally went—my dad used to warn me about old houses doing that kind of thing—but then I touch it and it’s dry.\n\nThe rest is more hearing it… the water running when no faucet’s on, and those men whispering inside the walls when there’s nobody there. That’s what scared my wife, I think.\n\nDoctor: Are the sounds of water running and men whispering in the walls happening all the time, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: They come and go… I mean, it’s not every single second, but it happens enough that I’m always kind of listening for it. The water running, especially—that’ll start up and I’ll go check the faucets, the bathroom, under the sink, all that. Old houses make noises, sure, my dad used to go on about pipes settling and pressure and all that, but this feels… different.\n\nAnd the whispering, the men in the walls, that comes and goes too. Sometimes it’s quiet and then suddenly it’s like they’re back there again. That’s what finally scared my wife, I think—me checking and checking when nobody’s there, and then that dark wet-looking patch I see on the hallway floor sometimes, even though when I touch it, it’s dry.\n\nDoctor: Do these sounds and visions happen at any particular time or when you're feeling a certain way?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s not like a clock thing, no. I can’t say it’s always at night or after dinner or anything like that. It seems worse when I’m already feeling low, when the house stuff is on my mind and I’m just sitting there thinking how I’ve let everything go.\n\nBefore all that dragged on—before I got like this, before I stopped caring about the things I used to keep up with—I didn’t hear men in the walls or see those wet spots. I didn’t think people next door were watching me or tracking me. That all came after I already felt like I’d failed at everything.\n\nI don’t know… my dad used to say houses tell you when something’s wrong, pipes knocking and all that, and I keep thinking maybe I should’ve caught it sooner. But it wasn’t there before. Not like this.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the sounds and visions stop or change when your mood improves?\n\nPatient: I think so… or, well, I don’t have a lot of good days to compare it to lately. But before the house problems dragged on and I got this low, I wasn’t hearing voices in the walls or seeing those wet-looking patches that weren’t really wet.\n\nIt all showed up after I’d already kind of stopped caring about things—my usual stuff, the house, everything—and started feeling like I’d failed every single day. Before that, I didn’t think the neighbors were tracking me either. That all came later. So… yeah, I guess when my mood’s worse, that’s when it seems to be there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or stressed when you hear the water running or the men whispering in the walls?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I mean, how could I not? When I hear that water running, I start thinking something’s leaking somewhere, and I go checking the faucets, the walls, the hallway… and then sometimes there’s that dark wet-looking patch spreading across the floor, but when I touch it, it’s dry.\n\nThe whispering is worse in a way. Men in the walls… nobody’s there, but I hear it, and that’s what really scared my wife, I think. Me stopping everything to listen, checking again and again. It makes me tense, like I’m waiting for something to happen.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that happens right before you start hearing the water running or the men whispering in the walls?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there’s one specific thing right before it. Sometimes I’m just standing there in the hallway, or checking something because I thought I heard a drip, and then it’s like the water is running somewhere in the walls. And then I’ll hear the men whispering… like inside there, but nobody’s there.\n\nWhat scared my wife, I think, was me stopping and listening like that, checking over and over. And sometimes I see this dark wet patch spreading across the hallway floor, but when I go touch it, it’s dry. It’s always dry. Which doesn’t make sense, because it looks wet. My dad used to know about these things with houses, leaks and bad pipes, but… I don’t know. I can’t tell what starts it exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant traumatic events in your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, “traumatic” is a big word. I wouldn’t know what counts exactly. Nothing like a… I don’t know, some big obvious thing, not that I can point to right now.\n\nMy dad was the kind of person who always knew how to fix things around the house, and I think about that more lately, but that’s not really what you asked. I guess I’m not sure. Nothing specific comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Has anything upsetting or disturbing happened to you recently that you think might be connected to these experiences?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, maybe the house. The winter freeze, that’s when everything turned into this mess with repairs, and I couldn’t get out from under it. It’s been, what, about seven months now?\n\nI keep thinking if I’d handled it better, or if I knew more like my dad did with fixing things, maybe it wouldn’t have gotten so bad. But it was after that freeze, after the house stuff started piling up, that all this really seemed to start.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were outside of your body watching yourself, or like you were in a dream?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not exactly. I mean, I get kind of out of it sometimes, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as being outside my body.\n\nLike, I’ll be standing there and realize I’ve been staring too long, and then I start thinking about how I used to do that as a kid in school, just looking at the clock and losing track, and the teacher would call on me and I’d have no idea what page we were on. But that’s different, I guess.\n\nA dream feeling… maybe I don’t know what you mean by that. I haven’t really noticed watching myself from outside or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were in a dream or detached from the world around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think I’d say “in a dream,” exactly. Detached, maybe if you mean kind of spaced out, but not like the world isn’t real or I’m not in it.\n\nSometimes I just get stuck thinking, like my mind wanders off, and then I’m thinking about something completely different—like an errand I forgot or some thing in the house that still needs fixing—and I realize I wasn’t really paying attention. But I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I haven’t noticed anything like being separate from the world, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the problems with the house, like the winter freeze and the repairs, triggered these experiences with the sounds and visions?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it all kind of started feeling tied to that. The freeze, the pipes, all the repairs… and then I’d be sitting there and hear water running when there wasn’t any, or these men whispering, like inside the walls.\n\nThat’s what really scared my wife, I think. And sometimes I see this dark, wet-looking patch spreading in the hallway, and I’ll go check it, and it’s dry. But I still see it for a bit. It makes me feel like the house is still… I don’t know, like it’s not done with me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the sounds and visions would stop if the house repairs were finally completed and everything was back to normal?\n\nPatient: I want to think they would stop, yeah… if the house was just normal again. But I don’t really know. Because even after I check and it’s dry, I still see that dark wet patch spreading down the hallway for a bit.\n\nAnd the sounds… that’s what really scared my wife, I think. I keep hearing water running, or men whispering inside the walls, when there’s nobody there. I tell myself it’s just the repairs or the pipes, but then I go listen and there’s nothing. So… I hope fixing everything would make it stop, but I’m not sure anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the sounds and visions happen more often when you are feeling stressed or anxious about something specific?\n\nPatient: I think… yeah, probably. When I’m wound up about the house, that’s when I notice it more, or maybe I’m just listening for it more. It’s hard to tell.\n\nWhat finally scared my wife was that I kept hearing water running when there wasn’t any, and sometimes men whispering inside the walls. I’d go over and put my ear close, like some idiot, and there’s nobody there. And then with the hallway, I’ll see this dark wet patch spreading across it, even after I already checked and it’s dry.\n\nI guess stress makes me pay attention to it, but it feels like it’s happening on its own too.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not really connected to what's happening around you when you hear the whispers or see the wet patch?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t know if I’d call it “not connected.” I mean, when it happens I get kind of locked onto it, like everything narrows down to that spot or that sound.\n\nIt’s funny, I used to be good at tuning things out — when I was working, there’d be machines going, people talking, and I could still focus. Now it’s like one little thing in the house pulls me in and I lose track of what I was doing. But whether that’s disconnected or just scared… I don’t really know what the difference is.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Now it’s like one little thing in the house pulls me in and I lose track of what I was doing. But whether that’s disconnected or just scared… I don’t really know what the difference is.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you ever feel like you're not really yourself when you're hearing the whispers or seeing the wet patch?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know… “not myself” is hard to answer. I still feel like me, I guess, but I get so caught up in it that I’m not thinking normally.

No, I haven’t really noticed feeling like a different person. More like… scared and focused on it.

    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF23, F44.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… my wife kind of made me come in. She got scared because I keep hearing this water running, like a faucet’s on somewhere, and sometimes men whispering inside the walls. I keep checking, you know, like under the sink, around the bathroom—our house is old, the pipes have always been weird, my dad used to say you can tell a lot about a house by the noises it makes—but there’s nobody there.\n\nAnd sometimes I’ll see this dark, wet-looking patch spreading across the hallway floor, like water seeping out, but when I go over and touch it, it’s dry. That’s… that’s what finally made her say I needed to talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Are the whispers you hear inside the walls the only thing you've been hearing, or have you been hearing any other sounds that aren't there?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly the water… the sound of water running, like somebody left a tap open somewhere. I’ll get up and check the sink, the bathroom, the pipes—my father was always fixing pipes when I was a kid, so I notice those noises—but there won’t be anything.\n\nAnd then there are the men whispering in the walls, yeah. I can’t always make out what they’re saying, but it’s like they’re back there. My wife says she doesn’t hear it. That’s what scared her, especially when I told her about the dark wet-looking patch I sometimes see on the hallway floor… but when I touch it, it’s dry.\n\nDoctor: Are the whispers and the sound of water the only things you're experiencing that aren't really there, or are you seeing things that aren't there besides the dark patch on the floor?\n\nPatient: No, not really besides that patch… I mean, that’s the main thing I see. It looks like this dark, wet spot spreading out on the hallway floor, like the wood’s soaking up water, and I’ll think, oh great, the pipes finally went—my dad used to warn me about old houses doing that kind of thing—but then I touch it and it’s dry.\n\nThe rest is more hearing it… the water running when no faucet’s on, and those men whispering inside the walls when there’s nobody there. That’s what scared my wife, I think.\n\nDoctor: Are the sounds of water running and men whispering in the walls happening all the time, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: They come and go… I mean, it’s not every single second, but it happens enough that I’m always kind of listening for it. The water running, especially—that’ll start up and I’ll go check the faucets, the bathroom, under the sink, all that. Old houses make noises, sure, my dad used to go on about pipes settling and pressure and all that, but this feels… different.\n\nAnd the whispering, the men in the walls, that comes and goes too. Sometimes it’s quiet and then suddenly it’s like they’re back there again. That’s what finally scared my wife, I think—me checking and checking when nobody’s there, and then that dark wet-looking patch I see on the hallway floor sometimes, even though when I touch it, it’s dry.\n\nDoctor: Do these sounds and visions happen at any particular time or when you're feeling a certain way?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s not like a clock thing, no. I can’t say it’s always at night or after dinner or anything like that. It seems worse when I’m already feeling low, when the house stuff is on my mind and I’m just sitting there thinking how I’ve let everything go.\n\nBefore all that dragged on—before I got like this, before I stopped caring about the things I used to keep up with—I didn’t hear men in the walls or see those wet spots. I didn’t think people next door were watching me or tracking me. That all came after I already felt like I’d failed at everything.\n\nI don’t know… my dad used to say houses tell you when something’s wrong, pipes knocking and all that, and I keep thinking maybe I should’ve caught it sooner. But it wasn’t there before. Not like this.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the sounds and visions stop or change when your mood improves?\n\nPatient: I think so… or, well, I don’t have a lot of good days to compare it to lately. But before the house problems dragged on and I got this low, I wasn’t hearing voices in the walls or seeing those wet-looking patches that weren’t really wet.\n\nIt all showed up after I’d already kind of stopped caring about things—my usual stuff, the house, everything—and started feeling like I’d failed every single day. Before that, I didn’t think the neighbors were tracking me either. That all came later. So… yeah, I guess when my mood’s worse, that’s when it seems to be there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or stressed when you hear the water running or the men whispering in the walls?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I mean, how could I not? When I hear that water running, I start thinking something’s leaking somewhere, and I go checking the faucets, the walls, the hallway… and then sometimes there’s that dark wet-looking patch spreading across the floor, but when I touch it, it’s dry.\n\nThe whispering is worse in a way. Men in the walls… nobody’s there, but I hear it, and that’s what really scared my wife, I think. Me stopping everything to listen, checking again and again. It makes me tense, like I’m waiting for something to happen.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that happens right before you start hearing the water running or the men whispering in the walls?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there’s one specific thing right before it. Sometimes I’m just standing there in the hallway, or checking something because I thought I heard a drip, and then it’s like the water is running somewhere in the walls. And then I’ll hear the men whispering… like inside there, but nobody’s there.\n\nWhat scared my wife, I think, was me stopping and listening like that, checking over and over. And sometimes I see this dark wet patch spreading across the hallway floor, but when I go touch it, it’s dry. It’s always dry. Which doesn’t make sense, because it looks wet. My dad used to know about these things with houses, leaks and bad pipes, but… I don’t know. I can’t tell what starts it exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant traumatic events in your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, “traumatic” is a big word. I wouldn’t know what counts exactly. Nothing like a… I don’t know, some big obvious thing, not that I can point to right now.\n\nMy dad was the kind of person who always knew how to fix things around the house, and I think about that more lately, but that’s not really what you asked. I guess I’m not sure. Nothing specific comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Has anything upsetting or disturbing happened to you recently that you think might be connected to these experiences?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, maybe the house. The winter freeze, that’s when everything turned into this mess with repairs, and I couldn’t get out from under it. It’s been, what, about seven months now?\n\nI keep thinking if I’d handled it better, or if I knew more like my dad did with fixing things, maybe it wouldn’t have gotten so bad. But it was after that freeze, after the house stuff started piling up, that all this really seemed to start.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were outside of your body watching yourself, or like you were in a dream?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not exactly. I mean, I get kind of out of it sometimes, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as being outside my body.\n\nLike, I’ll be standing there and realize I’ve been staring too long, and then I start thinking about how I used to do that as a kid in school, just looking at the clock and losing track, and the teacher would call on me and I’d have no idea what page we were on. But that’s different, I guess.\n\nA dream feeling… maybe I don’t know what you mean by that. I haven’t really noticed watching myself from outside or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were in a dream or detached from the world around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think I’d say “in a dream,” exactly. Detached, maybe if you mean kind of spaced out, but not like the world isn’t real or I’m not in it.\n\nSometimes I just get stuck thinking, like my mind wanders off, and then I’m thinking about something completely different—like an errand I forgot or some thing in the house that still needs fixing—and I realize I wasn’t really paying attention. But I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I haven’t noticed anything like being separate from the world, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the problems with the house, like the winter freeze and the repairs, triggered these experiences with the sounds and visions?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it all kind of started feeling tied to that. The freeze, the pipes, all the repairs… and then I’d be sitting there and hear water running when there wasn’t any, or these men whispering, like inside the walls.\n\nThat’s what really scared my wife, I think. And sometimes I see this dark, wet-looking patch spreading in the hallway, and I’ll go check it, and it’s dry. But I still see it for a bit. It makes me feel like the house is still… I don’t know, like it’s not done with me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the sounds and visions would stop if the house repairs were finally completed and everything was back to normal?\n\nPatient: I want to think they would stop, yeah… if the house was just normal again. But I don’t really know. Because even after I check and it’s dry, I still see that dark wet patch spreading down the hallway for a bit.\n\nAnd the sounds… that’s what really scared my wife, I think. I keep hearing water running, or men whispering inside the walls, when there’s nobody there. I tell myself it’s just the repairs or the pipes, but then I go listen and there’s nothing. So… I hope fixing everything would make it stop, but I’m not sure anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the sounds and visions happen more often when you are feeling stressed or anxious about something specific?\n\nPatient: I think… yeah, probably. When I’m wound up about the house, that’s when I notice it more, or maybe I’m just listening for it more. I’d go over and put my ear close, like some idiot, and there’s nobody there. Now it’s like one little thing in the house pulls me in and I lose track of what I was doing. But whether that’s disconnected or just scared… I don’t really know what the difference is.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not really yourself when you're hearing the whispers or seeing the wet patch?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “not myself” is hard to answer. I still feel like me, I guess, but I get so caught up in it that I’m not thinking normally.\n\nLike, I’ll be standing there listening, and then I remember I was supposed to be doing something else. My wife used to say I’d get that way even with small things, like fixing a cabinet hinge for two hours because one screw wouldn’t sit right. Sorry, that’s not what you asked.\n\nNo, I haven’t really noticed feeling like a different person. More like… scared and focused on it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I don’t think it’s like reliving a memory, no. At least not the way I understand that.

Sometimes the wet patch makes me think of other things, like leaks we had in an old apartment, and then I’ll start worrying about the wall or the ceiling, and I can get stuck on that for a while. But that’s more me thinking, not like I’m back there or seeing some old event happen again.

The whispers… I don’t know. I’m listening to them in the moment. It doesn’t feel like a memory playing. It just feels like it’s happening then, if that makes sense.

Sometimes it’s quiet and then suddenly it’s like they’re back there again. That’s what finally scared my wife, I think—me checking and checking when nobody’s there, and then that dark wet-looking patch I see on the hallway floor sometimes, even though when I touch it, it’s dry.\n\nDoctor: Do these sounds and visions happen at any particular time or when you're feeling a certain way?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s not like a clock thing, no. I can’t say it’s always at night or after dinner or anything like that. It seems worse when I’m already feeling low, when the house stuff is on my mind and I’m just sitting there thinking how I’ve let everything go.\n\nBefore all that dragged on—before I got like this, before I stopped caring about the things I used to keep up with—I didn’t hear men in the walls or see those wet spots. I didn’t think people next door were watching me or tracking me. That all came after I already felt like I’d failed at everything.\n\nI don’t know… my dad used to say houses tell you when something’s wrong, pipes knocking and all that, and I keep thinking maybe I should’ve caught it sooner. But it wasn’t there before. Not like this.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the sounds and visions stop or change when your mood improves?\n\nPatient: I think so… or, well, I don’t have a lot of good days to compare it to lately. But before the house problems dragged on and I got this low, I wasn’t hearing voices in the walls or seeing those wet-looking patches that weren’t really wet.\n\nIt all showed up after I’d already kind of stopped caring about things—my usual stuff, the house, everything—and started feeling like I’d failed every single day. Before that, I didn’t think the neighbors were tracking me either. That all came later. So… yeah, I guess when my mood’s worse, that’s when it seems to be there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or stressed when you hear the water running or the men whispering in the walls?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I mean, how could I not? When I hear that water running, I start thinking something’s leaking somewhere, and I go checking the faucets, the walls, the hallway… and then sometimes there’s that dark wet-looking patch spreading across the floor, but when I touch it, it’s dry.\n\nThe whispering is worse in a way. Men in the walls… nobody’s there, but I hear it, and that’s what really scared my wife, I think. Me stopping everything to listen, checking again and again. It makes me tense, like I’m waiting for something to happen.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that happens right before you start hearing the water running or the men whispering in the walls?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there’s one specific thing right before it. Sometimes I’m just standing there in the hallway, or checking something because I thought I heard a drip, and then it’s like the water is running somewhere in the walls. And then I’ll hear the men whispering… like inside there, but nobody’s there.\n\nWhat scared my wife, I think, was me stopping and listening like that, checking over and over. And sometimes I see this dark wet patch spreading across the hallway floor, but when I go touch it, it’s dry. It’s always dry. Which doesn’t make sense, because it looks wet. My dad used to know about these things with houses, leaks and bad pipes, but… I don’t know. I can’t tell what starts it exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant traumatic events in your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, “traumatic” is a big word. I wouldn’t know what counts exactly. Nothing like a… I don’t know, some big obvious thing, not that I can point to right now.\n\nMy dad was the kind of person who always knew how to fix things around the house, and I think about that more lately, but that’s not really what you asked. I guess I’m not sure. Nothing specific comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Has anything upsetting or disturbing happened to you recently that you think might be connected to these experiences?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, maybe the house. The winter freeze, that’s when everything turned into this mess with repairs, and I couldn’t get out from under it. It’s been, what, about seven months now?\n\nI keep thinking if I’d handled it better, or if I knew more like my dad did with fixing things, maybe it wouldn’t have gotten so bad. But it was after that freeze, after the house stuff started piling up, that all this really seemed to start.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were outside of your body watching yourself, or like you were in a dream?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not exactly. I mean, I get kind of out of it sometimes, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as being outside my body.\n\nLike, I’ll be standing there and realize I’ve been staring too long, and then I start thinking about how I used to do that as a kid in school, just looking at the clock and losing track, and the teacher would call on me and I’d have no idea what page we were on. But that’s different, I guess.\n\nA dream feeling… maybe I don’t know what you mean by that. I haven’t really noticed watching myself from outside or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were in a dream or detached from the world around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think I’d say “in a dream,” exactly. Detached, maybe if you mean kind of spaced out, but not like the world isn’t real or I’m not in it.\n\nSometimes I just get stuck thinking, like my mind wanders off, and then I’m thinking about something completely different—like an errand I forgot or some thing in the house that still needs fixing—and I realize I wasn’t really paying attention. But I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I haven’t noticed anything like being separate from the world, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the problems with the house, like the winter freeze and the repairs, triggered these experiences with the sounds and visions?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it all kind of started feeling tied to that. The freeze, the pipes, all the repairs… and then I’d be sitting there and hear water running when there wasn’t any, or these men whispering, like inside the walls.\n\nThat’s what really scared my wife, I think. And sometimes I see this dark, wet-looking patch spreading in the hallway, and I’ll go check it, and it’s dry. But I still see it for a bit. It makes me feel like the house is still… I don’t know, like it’s not done with me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the sounds and visions would stop if the house repairs were finally completed and everything was back to normal?\n\nPatient: I want to think they would stop, yeah… if the house was just normal again. But I don’t really know. Because even after I check and it’s dry, I still see that dark wet patch spreading down the hallway for a bit.\n\nAnd the sounds… that’s what really scared my wife, I think. I keep hearing water running, or men whispering inside the walls, when there’s nobody there. I tell myself it’s just the repairs or the pipes, but then I go listen and there’s nothing. So… I hope fixing everything would make it stop, but I’m not sure anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the sounds and visions happen more often when you are feeling stressed or anxious about something specific?\n\nPatient: I think… yeah, probably. When I’m wound up about the house, that’s when I notice it more, or maybe I’m just listening for it more. It’s hard to tell.\n\nWhat finally scared my wife was that I kept hearing water running when there wasn’t any, and sometimes men whispering inside the walls. I’d go over and put my ear close, like some idiot, and there’s nobody there. And then with the hallway, I’ll see this dark wet patch spreading across it, even after I already checked and it’s dry.\n\nI guess stress makes me pay attention to it, but it feels like it’s happening on its own too.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not really connected to what's happening around you when you hear the whispers or see the wet patch?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t know if I’d call it “not connected.” I mean, when it happens I get kind of locked onto it, like everything narrows down to that spot or that sound.\n\nIt’s funny, I used to be good at tuning things out — when I was working, there’d be machines going, people talking, and I could still focus. Now it’s like one little thing in the house pulls me in and I lose track of what I was doing. But whether that’s disconnected or just scared… I don’t really know what the difference is.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not really yourself when you're hearing the whispers or seeing the wet patch?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “not myself” is hard to answer. I still feel like me, I guess, but I get so caught up in it that I’m not thinking normally.\n\nLike, I’ll be standing there listening, and then I remember I was supposed to be doing something else. My wife used to say I’d get that way even with small things, like fixing a cabinet hinge for two hours because one screw wouldn’t sit right. Sorry, that’s not what you asked.\n\nNo, I haven’t really noticed feeling like a different person. More like… scared and focused on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're observing yourself from outside your body when you hear the whispers or see the wet patch?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like I’m floating above myself or watching from the ceiling or anything. I mean, sometimes I’ll feel strange after, like embarrassed almost, because I’ll realize I’ve been standing there listening for too long.\n\nBut outside my body… no, I haven’t noticed that. My brother used to talk about something like that after he had surgery once, with the anesthesia—sorry, that’s not really related. I just mean I know where I am. I’m there in the hallway or by the wall, just… too focused on it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you are reliving a memory when you hear the whispers or see the wet patch?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like reliving a memory, no. At least not the way I understand that.\n\nSometimes the wet patch makes me think of other things, like leaks we had in an old apartment, and then I’ll start worrying about the wall or the ceiling, and I can get stuck on that for a while. But that’s more me thinking, not like I’m back there or seeing some old event happen again.\n\nThe whispers… I don’t know. I’m listening to them in the moment. It doesn’t feel like a memory playing. It just feels like it’s happening then, if that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

And then I’ll hear the men whispering… like inside there, but nobody’s there.\n\nWhat scared my wife, I think, was me stopping and listening like that, checking over and over. I guess I’m not sure. Nothing specific comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Has anything upsetting or disturbing happened to you recently that you think might be connected to these experiences?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, maybe the house. The winter freeze, that’s when everything turned into this mess with repairs, and I couldn’t get out from under it. It’s been, what, about seven months now?\n\nI keep thinking if I’d handled it better, or if I knew more like my dad did with fixing things, maybe it wouldn’t have gotten so bad. But it was after that freeze, after the house stuff started piling up, that all this really seemed to start.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were outside of your body watching yourself, or like you were in a dream?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not exactly. I mean, I get kind of out of it sometimes, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as being outside my body.\n\nLike, I’ll be standing there and realize I’ve been staring too long, and then I start thinking about how I used to do that as a kid in school, just looking at the clock and losing track, and the teacher would call on me and I’d have no idea what page we were on. I haven’t really noticed watching myself from outside or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were in a dream or detached from the world around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think I’d say “in a dream,” exactly. I haven’t noticed anything like being separate from the world, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the problems with the house, like the winter freeze and the repairs, triggered these experiences with the sounds and visions?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it all kind of started feeling tied to that. The freeze, the pipes, all the repairs… and then I’d be sitting there and hear water running when there wasn’t any, or these men whispering, like inside the walls.\n\nThat’s what really scared my wife, I think.
